## Translator's report

**Overall approach**
- **Formal "usted" throughout** (*Conecte*, *Vuelva a intentarlo*, *su espacio de trabajo*). This fits a clinical, formal voice for engineering managers. Some developer brands in Latin America use "tú" instead. If you ever switch, it's a find-and-replace across the verbs and possessives, but every string has to change together.
- **Neutral Latin American Spanish.** There are no voseo forms and no regional words. I used *computadora*-style vocabulary rather than peninsular Spanish (no *ordenador*, no *vosotros*).
- **Commands as bare infinitives** (*Guardar*, *Eliminar*), which is standard Spanish UI practice. **Sentence case** throughout, following Spanish capitalization rules.
- **All placeholders are kept exactly:** `{{count}}`, `{{seconds}}`, `{{email}}`.

**Character budget**

| Key | EN | ES | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| button.save | 12 | 15 | ✅ |
| button.delete | 6 | 8 | ✅ |
| button.cancel | 6 | 8 | ✅ |
| button.invite | 15 | 17 | ✅ |
| confirm.delete.title | 30 | 34 | With a count of 12, it renders at 27 characters vs. 23 in English. This is the shortest natural wording. |

**Decisions to review**
1. **`button.invite` → "Invitar al equipo".** A literal *"Invitar a un compañero"* (22 characters) fits the limit. But *compañero* is grammatically masculine, and *compañero/a* looks awkward on a button. "Invitar al equipo" avoids gender and is common SaaS wording. If you want something closer to "teammate", use *"Invitar a un miembro"* (20).
2. **`error.rate_limit`.** I used an impersonal "Se alcanzó…" in place of "You've hit…". It reads more clinical and doesn't blame the user. I chose *límite de solicitudes* over *límite de frecuencia* or *velocidad* because it's the clearest of the three for both managers and developers.
3. **`empty_state.body`.** I translated "your stack" as *entre sus sistemas*. Many Latin American developers say *stack* in English. If your audience leans technical, *"en todo su stack"* is also acceptable.
4. **Terms left in English on purpose:** *endpoint*, *webhook*, *API*, and `Authorization`. `Authorization` is the literal HTTP header name, so it must not be translated. *API* is feminine in Spanish (*su primera API*).
5. **`error.rate_limit` "en un plazo máximo de"** carries the hard-deadline meaning of "within".

**Engineering flags (these affect the source strings too)**
- **Plurals:** `confirm.delete.*` and `error.rate_limit` break when the number is 1 (*"¿Eliminar 1 integraciones?"*, *"1 segundos"*). The English has the same bug. I recommend ICU plural syntax, for example: `{count, plural, one {¿Eliminar # integración?} other {¿Eliminar # integraciones?}}`. I can send the singular forms if you adopt it.
- **Expansion:** Running text comes out about 15–25% longer in Spanish. Check `confirm.delete.body` and `webhook.help` in fixed-width modals and tooltips.

Prompt

Localize these UI strings for our B2B SaaS product to neutral Latin American Spanish. The product is a developer platform for managing API integrations — clinical, formal brand voice (think Stripe, not Mailchimp). Audience is engineering managers and senior devs. Preserve all placeholders. Button labels and confirmation titles should fit in roughly the same character budget as English (max 25 chars for buttons).

{
  "button.save": "Save changes",
  "button.delete": "Delete",
  "button.cancel": "Cancel",
  "button.invite": "Invite teammate",
  "confirm.delete.title": "Delete {{count}} integrations?",
  "confirm.delete.body": "This will permanently remove {{count}} integrations from your workspace. This action cannot be undone.",
  "error.rate_limit": "You've hit the rate limit. Try again in {{seconds}} seconds.",
  "empty_state.title": "No integrations yet",
  "empty_state.body": "Connect your first API to start syncing data across your stack.",
  "toast.invite_sent": "Invite sent to {{email}}",
  "webhook.help": "Your webhook endpoint must return a 2xx status code within 5 seconds.",
  "settings.api_key.label": "API key",
  "settings.api_key.help": "Use this key in the Authorization header of your requests."
}

Return the translated JSON in the same shape, and include the translator's report.
